21. {
22.     public partial class MainPage : Page
23.     {
24.         public const string FEATURE_NAME = "AdaptiveStreaming";
25. 
26.         List<Scenario> scenarios = new List<Scenario>
27.         {
28.             new Scenario() { Title="Simplest Adaptive Streaming", ClassType=typeof(Scenario1_SimplestAdaptiveStreaming)},
29.             new Scenario() { Title="Event Handlers", ClassType=typeof(Scenario2_EventHandlers)},
30.             new Scenario() { Title="Network Request Modification", ClassType=typeof(Scenario3_RequestModification)},
31.             new Scenario() { Title="Adaptive Streaming Tuning", ClassType=typeof(Scenario4_Tuning)},
32.             new Scenario() { Title="Metadata", ClassType=typeof(Scenario5_Metadata)},
33.             new Scenario() { Title="Ad Insertion", ClassType=typeof(Scenario6_AdInsertion)},
34.             new Scenario() { Title="Live Seekable Range", ClassType=typeof(Scenario7_LiveSeekableRange)},
35.         };
36. 
37.         public static IReadOnlyList<AdaptiveContentModel> ContentManagementSystemStub = AdaptiveContentModel.GetKnownAzureMediaServicesModels();
38.         public static AdaptiveContentModel FindContentById(int id)
39.         {
40.             return ContentManagementSystemStub.First(model => model.Id == id);
41.         }
42.     }
43. 
44.     public class Scenario
45.     {
46.         public string Title { get; set; }
47.         public Type ClassType { get; set; }
48.     }
49. 
50.     public class BitrateHelper
51.     {
52.         // Each bitrate is assigned a symbol as follows:
53.         // The bottom quartile gets 1 bar, the second quartile gets 2 bars,
54.         // the third quartile gets 3 bars, and the top quartile gets 4 bars.
55.         private Dictionary<uint, Symbol> symbolAssignment = new Dictionary<uint, Symbol>();
56. 
57.         public BitrateHelper(IEnumerable<uint> availableBitRates)
58.         {
59.             uint[] sortedBitRates = availableBitRates.ToArray();
60.             Array.Sort(sortedBitRates);
61.             int i = 0;
62.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 1 / 4)
63.             {
64.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.OneBar);
65.             }
66.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 2 / 4)
67.             {
68.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.TwoBars);
69.             }
70.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 3 / 4)
71.             {
72.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.ThreeBars);
73.             }
74.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 4 / 4)
75.             {
76.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.FourBars);
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         public Symbol GetBitrateSymbol(uint currentBitrate)
81.         {
82.             Symbol symbol = Symbol.ZeroBars;
83.             if (symbolAssignment.TryGetValue(currentBitrate, out symbol))
84.             {
85.                 return symbol;
86.             }
87.             return Symbol.ZeroBars;
88.         }
89.     }
90. }

95.         private async void LoadId_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
96.         {
97.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
98.                 return;
99. 
100.             this.SelectedModel = (AdaptiveContentModel)SelectedContent.SelectedItem;
101. 
102.             UriBox.Text = SelectedModel.ManifestUri.ToString();
103.             await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(SelectedModel.ManifestUri);
104.         }

106.         private async void LoadUri_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
107.         {
108.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
109.                 return;
110. 
111.             Uri uri;
112.             if (!Uri.TryCreate(UriBox.Text, UriKind.Absolute, out uri))
113.             {
114.                 loggerControl.Log("Malformed Uri in Load text box.", SDKTemplate.Logging.LogViewLoggingLevel.Critical);
115.                 return;
116.             }
117.             await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(uri);
118.         }
119. 
120.         private async Task CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(Uri uri)
121.         {
122.             SetSourceEnabled(false);
123.             this.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
124.             this.MediaPlaybackItem = await CreateMediaPlaybackItem(uri, optionalHttpClient);
125.             if (this.MediaPlaybackItem != null)
126.             {
127.                 loggerControl.Log($"Loaded Uri: {uri}");
128.                 SetSourceEnabled(true);
129.             }
130.         }

142.         {

144.                 return;
145. 
146.             mediaPlayer.AutoPlay = (bool)(sender as CheckBox).IsChecked;
147.         }
148. 
149.         private void ForceEquirectangularSphericalVideoFrameFormat()
150.         {
151.             if (mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.None ||
152.                 mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.Opening)
153.                 return;
154. 
155.             var mpsvp = mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SphericalVideoProjection;
156.             if (mpsvp == null)
157.                 return;
158. 
159.             if (mpsvp.FrameFormat != Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular)
160.                 mpsvp.FrameFormat = Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular;
161. 
162.             if (mpsvp.IsEnabled != true)
163.                 mpsvp.IsEnabled = true;
164. 
165.             mpsvp.HorizontalFieldOfViewInDegrees = 120;
166.         }
167. 
168.         private void SetSource_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
169.         {
170.             if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
171.                 return;
172.             
173.             // It is at this point that the MediaSource (within a MediaPlaybackItem) will be fully resolved.
174.             // For an AdaptiveMediaSource, this is the point at which media is first requested and parsed.
175.             mediaPlayer.Source = this.MediaPlaybackItem;
176.         }

82.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             // Explicitly create the instance of MediaPlayer if you need to register for its events
85.             // (like MediaOpened / MediaFailed) prior to setting an IMediaPlaybackSource.
86.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
87. 
88.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
89.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
90. 
91.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
92.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
93.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
94.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // This Scenario focuses on event handling, mostly via the "Logger" helper classes
97.             // in the Shared folder.
98.             //
99.             // In addition, the App can also insert an IHttpFilter into the Windows.Web.Http stack
100.             // which is used by the AdaptiveMediaSource.  To do so, it must derive a class from
101.             // IHttpFilter, provide the HttpBaseProtocolFilter to that class to execute requests,
102.             // then use the derived filter as the constructor for the HttpClient.
103.             //
104.             // The HttpClient is then used in the constructor of the AdaptiveMediaSource.
105.             // The App should explicitly set:
106.             //   CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache
107.             // When passing an HttpClient into the constructor of AdpativeMediaSource,
108.             // set this on the HttpBaseProtocolFilter.
109.             //
110.             // More than one IHttpFilter can be nested, for example, an App might
111.             // use a filter to modify web requests: see Scenario3 for details.
112.             //
113.             // In this scenario, we add an AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger to provide additional
114.             // verbose logging details for each request.
115.             var baseProtocolFilter = new HttpBaseProtocolFilter();
116.             baseProtocolFilter.CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache;  // Always set WriteBehavior = NoCache
117.             var fistLevelFilter = new AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LoggerControl, baseProtocolFilter);
118.             var httpClient = new HttpClient(fistLevelFilter);
119. 
120. 
121.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
122.                 mediaPlayer,
123.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
124.                 httpClient,
125.                 LoggerControl,
126.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
127.         }
128. 
129. 
130.         #region Content Loading
131. 
132.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
133.         {
134.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
135. 
136.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
137.             if (httpClient != null)
138.             {
139.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
140.             }
141.             else
142.             {
143.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
144.             }
145. 
146.             MediaSource source;
147.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
148.             {
149.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
150. 
151.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
152.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
153. 
154.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
155.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
156. 
157.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
158.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
159. 
160.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource chooses initial playback and download bitrates.
161.                 // See the Tuning scenario for examples of customizing these bitrates.
162.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
163.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
164. 
165.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
166. 
167.             }
168.             else
169.             {
170.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
171. 
172.                 // In this scenario, we make a second attempt to load any URI that is
173.                 // not adaptive streaming as a progressive download URI:
174.                 Log($"Attempting to create a MediaSource from uri: {uri}");
175.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromUri(uri);
176.             }
177. 
178.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
179.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
180. 
181.             // You can save additional information in the CustomPropertySet for future retrieval.
182.             // Note: MediaSource.CustomProperties is a ValueSet and therefore can store
183.             // only serializable types.
184. 
185.             // Save the original Uri.
186.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
187. 
188.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
189.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
190. 
191.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
192. 
193.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
194.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
195. 
196.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
197. 
198.             return mpItem;
199.         }
200.

206.             {
207.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
208.             });
209.         }

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<exited with exit code 0>

45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)

50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }

53.

64.         /// <summary>

67.         /// </summary>

69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)

81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }

92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}

32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }

37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
39.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();

101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107.

16. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
17. {
18.     public class AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger : IDisposable
19.     {
20.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
21. 
22.         private LogView logView;
23.         private AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource;
24. 
25.         public AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LogView _logView, AdaptiveMediaSource _adaptiveMediaSource)
26.         {
27.             if (_logView == null)
28.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
29.             this.logView = _logView;
30. 
31.             if (_adaptiveMediaSource == null)
32.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_adaptiveMediaSource));
33.             this.adaptiveMediaSource = _adaptiveMediaSource;
34. 
35.             this.RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents();
36.         }
37. 
38.         public void Dispose()
39.         {
40.             this.UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents();
41. 
42.             this.logView = null;
43.             this.adaptiveMediaSource = null;
44.         }
45.         
46.         #endregion
47. 
48. 
49.         #region AdaptiveMediaSource Event Handlers
50. 
51.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents()
52.         {
53.             adaptiveMediaSource.Diagnostics.DiagnosticAvailable += DiagnosticAvailable;
54.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;
55.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadCompleted += DownloadCompleted;
56.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed += DownloadFailed;
57.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested += DownloadRequested;
58.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged += PlaybackBitrateChanged;
59.         }
60. 
61.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents()
62.         {
63.             if (adaptiveMediaSource == null)
64.             {
65.                 return;
66.             }
67.             adaptiveMediaSource.Diagnostics.DiagnosticAvailable -= DiagnosticAvailable;
68.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
69.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadCompleted -= DownloadCompleted;
70.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed -= DownloadFailed;
71.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested -= DownloadRequested;
72.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
73.         }
74. 
75.         private void DiagnosticAvailable(AdaptiveMediaSourceDiagnostics sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDiagnosticAvailableEventArgs args)
76.         {
77.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning);
78.         }
79. 
80.         private void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
81.         {
82.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
83.         }
84. 
85.         private void DownloadCompleted(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadCompletedEventArgs args)
86.         {
87.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
88.         }
89. 
90.         private void DownloadFailed(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadFailedEventArgs args)
91.         {
92.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
93.         }
94. 
95.         private void DownloadRequested(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadRequestedEventArgs args)
96.         {
97.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
98.         }

21. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
22. {
23.     /// <summary>
24.     /// Logging levels supported by the LogView control.
25.     /// The "Always" level is supported only by the Log method.
26.     /// </summary>
27.     public enum LogViewLoggingLevel
28.     {
29.         Verbose = 0,
30.         Information,
31.         Warning,
32.         Error,
33.         Critical,
34.         Always,
35.     }
36. 
37.     /// 
38.     /// <summary>
39.     /// This control can be used to log events from a scenario page.
40.     /// 
41.     /// In the XAML markup, you can set the initial logging level by setting
42.     /// LoggingLevel="Verbose", or any other enum value of LogViewLoggingLevel.
43.     /// </summary>
44.     public sealed partial class LogView : UserControl
45.     {
46.         public LogView()
47.         {
48.             this.InitializeComponent();
49. 
50.             LoggingBrushes = new SolidColorBrush[] {
51.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["VerboseLogBrush"],
52.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["InformationLogBrush"],
53.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["WarningLogBrush"],
54.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["ErrorLogBrush"],
55.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["CriticalLogBrush"],
56.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["AlwaysLogBrush"],
57.             };
58. 
59.             LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)LoggingLevel;
60.         }
61. 
62.         public static readonly DependencyProperty LogLevelProperty =
63.             DependencyProperty.Register("LoggingLevel", typeof(LogViewLoggingLevel), typeof(LogView),
64.                 new PropertyMetadata(LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning, OnLoggingLevelChanged));
65. 
66.         public LogViewLoggingLevel LoggingLevel
67.         {
68.             get { return (LogViewLoggingLevel)GetValue(LogLevelProperty); }
69.             set { SetValue(LogLevelProperty, value); }
70.         }
71. 
72.         private static void OnLoggingLevelChanged(DependencyObject d, DependencyPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
73.         {
74.             var logger = (LogView)d;
75.             var loggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)e.NewValue;
76.             logger.LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)loggingLevel;
77.         }
78. 
79.         bool IsLogViewFull = false;
80.         int MaxItemsInLogView = 500;
81. 
82.         SolidColorBrush[] LoggingBrushes;
83. 
84.         /// <summary>
85.         /// Changes the logging level at which messages are filtered.
86.         /// </summary>
87.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
88.         /// <param name="e"></param>
89.         private void LogLevel_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
90.         {
91.             LoggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex;
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// <summary>
95.         /// Provides logging with filtering based on UI settings.
96.         /// Logging functions tangential to main scenarios should specify a log view level less than Always.
97.         /// </summary>
98.         /// <param name="message">message to be logged</param>
99.         /// <param name="level">message category</param>
100.         public async void Log(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level = LogViewLoggingLevel.Always)
101.         {
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 LogFromUIThread(message, level);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
109.                 {
110.                     LogFromUIThread(message, level);
111.                 });
112.             }
113.         }
114. 
115.         private void LogFromUIThread(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level)
116.         {
117.             string logEntry = $"{DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss.fff} - {message}";
118. 
119.             if (LoggingLevel <= level)
120.             {
121.                 SolidColorBrush foregroundBrush = LoggingBrushes[(int)level];
122. 
123.                 if (IsLogViewFull)
124.                 {
125.                     // Once the view is full, messages go only to the debug console.
126.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
127.                     return;
128.                 }
129. 
130.                 if (LoggingListBox.Items.Count >= MaxItemsInLogView)
131.                 {
132.                     IsLogViewFull = true;
133. 
134.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
135.                     logEntry = "Log window is full";
136.                     foregroundBrush = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);
137.                 }
138. 
139.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Insert(0, new TextBlock() { Text = logEntry, Foreground = foregroundBrush });
140.             }
141.             else if (level != LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose)
142.             {
143.                 // Non-Verbose messages blocked from the LogView are shown on the debug console.
144.                 System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine($"{level}: {logEntry}");
145.             }
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void CopyLogToClipboard_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
149.         {
150.             var content = new DataPackage();
151.             content.SetText(String.Join("\r\n", LoggingListBox.Items.Select(item => (item as TextBlock).Text)));
152.             Clipboard.SetContent(content);
153.         }
154. 
155.         private void ClearLog_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
156.         {
157.             ClearLog();
158.         }
159. 
160.         public async void ClearLog()
161.         {
162.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
163.             {
164.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Clear();
165.                 IsLogViewFull = false;
166.             }
167.             else
168.             {
169.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, ClearLog);
170.             }
171.         }
172.     }
173. }

215.             logView.Log($"MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SupportedPlaybackRatesChanged, MinRate: {minRate}, MaxRate: {maxRate}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
216.         }
217. 
218.         private static void GetMinMaxRates(MediaPlaybackSession sender, out double minRate, out double maxRate)
219.         {
220.             minRate = -256.0;
221.             bool foundMinRate = false;
222.             for (; !foundMinRate; minRate = minRate / 2.0)
223.             {
224.                 foundMinRate = sender.IsSupportedPlaybackRateRange(minRate, 1);
225.                 if (-0.25 < minRate)
226.                 {
227.                     foundMinRate = true;
228.                     minRate = 0;
229.                 }
230.             }
231.             maxRate = 256.0;
232.             bool foundMaxRate = false;
233.             for (; !foundMaxRate; maxRate = maxRate / 2.0)
234.             {
235.                 foundMaxRate = sender.IsSupportedPlaybackRateRange(1, maxRate);
236.                 if (maxRate < 1.0)
237.                 {
238.                     foundMaxRate = true;
239.                     maxRate = 1;
240.                 }
241.             }
242.         }

12. using SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions;
13. using System;
14. using System.Diagnostics;
15. using System.Linq;
16. using System.Runtime.InteropServices.WindowsRuntime;
17. using Windows.Foundation;
18. using Windows.Web.Http;
19. using Windows.Web.Http.Filters;
20. 
21. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
22. {
23.     public class AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger : IHttpFilter
24.     {
25.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
26. 
27.         private LogView logView;
28.         private IHttpFilter innerFilter;
29.         private Stopwatch timeToResponseComplete;
30. 
31.         public AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LogView _logView, IHttpFilter _innerFilter)
32.         {
33.             if (_logView == null)
34.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
35.             this.logView = _logView;
36. 
37.             if (_innerFilter == null)
38.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_innerFilter));
39.             this.innerFilter = _innerFilter;
40. 
41.             this.timeToResponseComplete = new Stopwatch();
42.         }
43. 
44.         public void Dispose()
45.         {
46. 
47.             this.timeToResponseComplete = null;
48.             this.logView = null;
49.             this.innerFilter = null;
50.         }
51.         
52.         #endregion
53. 
54.         public IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> SendRequestAsync(HttpRequestMessage request)
55.         {
56.             timeToResponseComplete.Start();
57.             logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER SendRequestAsync\":{{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
58. 
59.             return AsyncInfo.Run<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(async (cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter) =>
60.             {
61.                 IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> innerOperation = innerFilter.SendRequestAsync(request);
62. 
63.                 innerOperation.Progress += new AsyncOperationProgressHandler<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(
64.                     (IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> asyncInfo, HttpProgress httpProgressInfo) =>
65.                 {
66.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Progress Handler\": {{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\", {httpProgressInfo.ToJsonObject()}}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
67.                 });
68. 
69.                 HttpResponseMessage response = await innerOperation.AsTask(cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter);
70.                 cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
71.                 // At this point, SendRequest has completed and we have a response.
72.                 timeToResponseComplete.Stop();
73. 
74.                 // We could inspect the content of the response headers:
75.                 var countHeaders = response.Headers.Count();
76.                 logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Complete\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
77. 
78.                 if(!response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
79.                 {
80.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Failed\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
81.                 }
82. 
83.                 // Note that we should NOT attempt to access the response.Content in an IHttpFilter such as this,
84.                 // the Content stream is for the base filter and AdaptiveMediaSource to use.
85.                 //
86.                 // If you want to download content in the App and then pass it to the AdaptiveMediaSource, 
87.                 // see AppDownloadedKeyRequest in Scenario3, which uses Deferral objects to manage thread timing
88.                 // with the AdaptiveMediaSource.
89.                 return response;
90.             });
91.         }
92. 
93.     }
94. }

Replace the NEXT line (and its block) from:\n   ```csharp\n   await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>\n   {\n       // code\n   });\n   ```\n   OR:\n   ```csharp\n   await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>\n   {\n       // code\n   }));\n   ```\n   TO:\n   ```csharp\n   DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>\n   {\n       // code\n   });\n   ```\n   Also make the containing method NOT `async` if the only `await` was the dispatcher call. uses `using Windows.UI.Core;` which is no longer needed since the bootstrap has already moved it to `migrate-as-is`. Also `Windows.ApplicationModel.DataTransfer` - that can stay. But `Symbol` type from `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls` - that's already handled by the namespace rewrite.\n\nRemove `using Windows.UI.Core;` from SampleConfiguration.cs since it's not used after migration.\n\n## Important: Method signature changes for async methods\n\nWhen fixing threading TODOs, check if the containing method is `async` ONLY because of the dispatcher call. If so:\n- Methods like `HideLoadUri()`, `SetAutoPlay()`, `SetSourceEnabled()` that are `async void` only to do the dispatcher call should become synchronous:\n  ```csharp\n  public void HideLoadUri()\n  {\n      DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>\n      {\n          this.LoadUriPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;\n      });\n  }\n  ```\n\n- Methods like `HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()`, `UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync()`, `UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync()` that are `async Task` or `async void` ONLY because of the dispatcher should become synchronous (return void, no async).\n\nBut be careful: If a method name ends in `Async` and returns `Task`, it's likely called with `await`. In that case, changing to synchronous may break callers. So check callers. Actually `UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync` and `UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync` in Scenario2 are called with `await` - so we can change them to return `void` and not use async, but then the callers won't await them anymore. The simplest fix is to just make the method not async and not await the dispatcher:\n\n```csharp\nprivate void UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(uint downloadBitrate)\n{\n    DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>\n    {\n        iconDownloadBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(downloadBitrate);\n        txtDownloadBitrate.Text = downloadBitrate.ToString();\n    });\n}\n```\nAnd change callers from `await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(...)` to just `UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(...)`. OR keep it async Task and return immediately after the TryEnqueue (since TryEnqueue is not awaitable). The simplest approach: keep the method async and just change the body - the `await` on TryEnqueue isn't possible, but you can just call it and return immediately. Actually the correct approach is:\n\nFor methods that ONLY do dispatcher work:\n1. Remove `async` keyword  \n2. Change return type from `Task` to `void`\n3. Update callers to remove `await`\n\nOR for backward compatibility:\n1. Keep async Task\n2. Replace `await dispatcher.RunAsync(...)` with `DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(...)` \n3. The method becomes `async Task` with no awaits, which is fine (no warning if you add `return Task.CompletedTask;` or just let it return implicitly)\n\nThe easiest approach that avoids breaking callers: just replace `await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, ...)` with `DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(...)` and leave the method signature as-is. A method that is `async Task` with no awaits will compile with a warning (CS1998) but that's acceptable.\n\n## Do NOT do:\n- Run git commands\n- Install new packages\n- Change files outside the target project\n\n## After making all changes:\nRun `winapp build` in the target project directory to check for build errors:\n```powershell\ncd \"B:\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run25\\AdaptiveStreaming_winui3-fromuwp-migration-user--DinahK-2SO--uwp2winui3-parity-agent__test-260618-131029_s46_i1\\app\\AdaptiveStreaming\"\nwinapp build\n```\nFix any build errors you find.     public class BitrateHelper
    {
        // Each bitrate is assigned a symbol as follows:
        // The bottom quartile gets 1 bar, the second quartile gets 2 bars,
        // the third quartile gets 3 bars, and the top quartile gets 4 bars.
        private Dictionary<uint, Symbol> symbolAssignment = new Dictionary<uint, Symbol>();        public BitrateHelper(IEnumerable<uint> availableBitRates)
        {
            uint[] sortedBitRates = availableBitRates.ToArray();
            Array.Sort(sortedBitRates);
            int i = 0;
            while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 1 / 4)
            {
                symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.OneBar);
            }
            while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 2 / 4)
            {
                symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.TwoBars);
            }
            while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 3 / 4)
            {
                symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.ThreeBars);
            }
            while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 4 / 4)
            {
                symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.FourBars);
            }
        }

        public Symbol GetBitrateSymbol(uint currentBitrate)
        {
            Symbol symbol = Symbol.ZeroBars;
            if (symbolAssignment.TryGetValue(currentBitrate, out symbol))
            {
                return symbol;
            }
            return Symbol.ZeroBars;
        }
    }
}

    public sealed partial class ContentSelector : UserControl
    {
        public ContentSelector()
        {
            this.InitializeComponent();
        }        private MediaPlayer mediaPlayer;
        private IEnumerable<AdaptiveContentModel> adaptiveContentModels;
        public AdaptiveContentModel SelectedModel;
        private LogView loggerControl;
        Func<Uri, HttpClient, Task<MediaPlaybackItem>> CreateMediaPlaybackItem;
        public MediaPlaybackItem MediaPlaybackItem;
        public HttpClient optionalHttpClient;        internal void Initialize(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer, 
            IEnumerable<AdaptiveContentModel> adaptiveContentModels,
            HttpClient optionalHttpClient,
            LogView loggerControl, 
            Func<Uri, HttpClient, Task<MediaPlaybackItem>> loadSourceFromUriAsync)
        {
            if (mediaPlayer == null)
                throw new ArgumentNullException("mediaPlayer");
            this.mediaPlayer = mediaPlayer;            if (adaptiveContentModels == null)
                throw new ArgumentNullException("adaptiveContentModels");
            this.adaptiveContentModels = adaptiveContentModels;            if (loggerControl == null)
                throw new ArgumentNullException("loggerControl");
            this.loggerControl = loggerControl;            if (loadSourceFromUriAsync == null)
                throw new ArgumentNullException("loadSourceFromUriAsync");
            this.CreateMediaPlaybackItem = loadSourceFromUriAsync;            this.optionalHttpClient = optionalHttpClient;            // Set data context:
            SelectedContent.ItemsSource = this.adaptiveContentModels;
            // Set default item:
            SetSelectedModel(this.adaptiveContentModels.First());
        }

        public void SetSelectedModel(AdaptiveContentModel model)
        {
            SelectedModel = model;
            SelectedContent.SelectedItem = SelectedModel;
        }

        public async void HideLoadUri()
        {
            // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                this.LoadUriPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
            });
        }
        public async void SetAutoPlay(bool IsChecked)
        {
            // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                AutoPlay.IsChecked = IsChecked;
            });
        }

        private async void LoadId_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
                return;

            this.SelectedModel = (AdaptiveContentModel)SelectedContent.SelectedItem;

            UriBox.Text = SelectedModel.ManifestUri.ToString();
            await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(SelectedModel.ManifestUri);
        }

        private async void LoadUri_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
                return;

            Uri uri;
            if (!Uri.TryCreate(UriBox.Text, UriKind.Absolute, out uri))
            {
                loggerControl.Log("Malformed Uri in Load text box.", SDKTemplate.Logging.LogViewLoggingLevel.Critical); 
                return;
            }
            await CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(uri);
        }

        private async Task CreateNewMediaPlaybackItem(Uri uri)
        {
            SetSourceEnabled(false);
            this.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
            this.MediaPlaybackItem = await CreateMediaPlaybackItem(uri, optionalHttpClient);
            if (this.MediaPlaybackItem != null)
            {
                loggerControl.Log($"Loaded Uri: {uri}");
                SetSourceEnabled(true);
            }
        }

        private async void SetSourceEnabled(bool SetEnabled)
        {
            // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                SetSource.IsEnabled = SetEnabled;
            });
        }

        private void AutoPlay_Checked(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
                return;

            mediaPlayer.AutoPlay = (bool)(sender as CheckBox).IsChecked;
        }

        private void ForceEquirectangularSphericalVideoFrameFormat()
        {
            if (mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.None ||
                mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackState == MediaPlaybackState.Opening)
                return;

            var mpsvp = mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SphericalVideoProjection;
            if (mpsvp == null)
                return;

            if (mpsvp.FrameFormat != Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular)
                mpsvp.FrameFormat = Windows.Media.MediaProperties.SphericalVideoFrameFormat.Equirectangular;

            if (mpsvp.IsEnabled != true)
                mpsvp.IsEnabled = true;

            mpsvp.HorizontalFieldOfViewInDegrees = 120;
        }

        private void SetSource_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (mediaPlayer == null || adaptiveContentModels == null || loggerControl == null)
                return;

            // It is at this point that the MediaSource (within a MediaPlaybackItem) will be fully resolved.
            // For an AdaptiveMediaSource, this is the point at which media is first requested and parsed.
            mediaPlayer.Source = this.MediaPlaybackItem;
        }
    }

//*********************************************************using SDKTemplate.Helpers;
using SDKTemplate.Logging;
using System;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Windows.Media.Core;
using Windows.Media.Playback;
using Windows.Media.Streaming.Adaptive;
using Windows.UI.Core;

using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
using Windows.Web.Http;
using Windows.Web.Http.Filters;namespace SDKTemplate
{
    /// See the README.md for discussion of this scenario.
    public sealed partial class Scenario2_EventHandlers : Page
    {
        private BitrateHelper bitrateHelper;        public Scenario2_EventHandlers()
        {
            this.InitializeComponent();
        }        protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
        {
            // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
            ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
            var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
            if (mediaPlayer != null)
            {
                mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
                mediaPlayerLogger = null;

                UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);                mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
                mediaPlayer.Dispose();
            }
        }        private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
        {
            AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
            MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
            if (mpItem != null)
            {
                adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
            }
            MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
            if (source != null)
            {
                adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
            }

            mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
            mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;

            mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
            mediaSourceLogger = null;

            adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
            adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;

            UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
        }

        private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            // Explicitly create the instance of MediaPlayer if you need to register for its events
            // (like MediaOpened / MediaFailed) prior to setting an IMediaPlaybackSource.
            var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();

            // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
            mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);

            // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
            var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
            prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
            mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);

            // This Scenario focuses on event handling, mostly via the "Logger" helper classes
            // in the Shared folder.
            //
            // In addition, the App can also insert an IHttpFilter into the Windows.Web.Http stack
            // which is used by the AdaptiveMediaSource.  To do so, it must derive a class from
            // IHttpFilter, provide the HttpBaseProtocolFilter to that class to execute requests,
            // then use the derived filter as the constructor for the HttpClient.
            //
            // The HttpClient is then used in the constructor of the AdaptiveMediaSource.
            // The App should explicitly set:
            //   CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache
            // When passing an HttpClient into the constructor of AdpativeMediaSource,
            // set this on the HttpBaseProtocolFilter.
            //
            // More than one IHttpFilter can be nested, for example, an App might
            // use a filter to modify web requests: see Scenario3 for details.
            //
            // In this scenario, we add an AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger to provide additional
            // verbose logging details for each request.
            var baseProtocolFilter = new HttpBaseProtocolFilter();
            baseProtocolFilter.CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache;  // Always set WriteBehavior
r = NoCache
            var fistLevelFilter = new AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LoggerControl, baseProtocolFilter);
            var httpClient = new HttpClient(fistLevelFilter);


            ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
                mediaPlayer,
                MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
                httpClient,
                LoggerControl,
                LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
        }


        #region Content Loading

        private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
        {
            UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);

            AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
            if (httpClient != null)
            {
                result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
            }
            else
            {
                result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
            }

            MediaSource source;
            if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
            {
                var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;

                // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
                adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);

                // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
                RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);

                // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
                bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);

                // The AdaptiveMediaSource chooses initial playback and download bitrates.
                // See the Tuning scenario for examples of customizing these bitrates.
                await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
                await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);

                source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);

            }
            else
            {
                Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.Ex
xtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");

                // In this scenario, we make a second attempt to load any URI that is
                // not adaptive streaming as a progressive download URI:
                Log($"Attempting to create a MediaSource from uri: {uri}");
                source = MediaSource.CreateFromUri(uri);
            }

            // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
            mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);

            // You can save additional information in the CustomPropertySet for future retrieval.
            // Note: MediaSource.CustomProperties is a ValueSet and therefore can store
            // only serializable types.

            // Save the original Uri.
            source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();

            // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
            source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();

            var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);

            // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
            mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);

            HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();

            return mpItem;
        }

        private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
        {
            // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
            // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
            });
        }
        #endregion


        #region AdaptiveMediaSource Event Handlers

        private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
        {
            adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;
            adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged += PlaybackBitrateChanged;
        }

        private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
        {
            if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
            {
                adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
                adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
            }
        }
        private async void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedE
EventArgs args)
        {
            uint downloadBitrate = args.NewValue;
            await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(downloadBitrate);
        }

        private async Task UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(uint downloadBitrate)
        {
            // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
            {
                iconDownloadBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(downloadBitrate);
                txtDownloadBitrate.Text = downloadBitrate.ToString();
            }));
        }

        private async void PlaybackBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourcePlaybackBitrateChangedE
EventArgs args)
        {
            uint playbackBitrate = args.NewValue;
            await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(playbackBitrate);
        }

        private async Task UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(uint playbackBitrate)
        {
            // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
            await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
            {
                iconPlaybackBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(playbackBitrate);
                txtPlaybackBitrate.Text = playbackBitrate.ToString();
            }));
        }
        #endregion


        #region Utilities
        private void Log(string message)
        {
            LoggerControl.Log(message);
        }
        MediaPlayerLogger mediaPlayerLogger;
        MediaSourceLogger mediaSourceLogger;
        MediaPlaybackItemLogger mediaPlaybackItemLogger;
        AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger adaptiveMediaSourceLogger;
        #endregion
    }
}

43.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh.Cancel(); // Cancel timer
46.             adaptiveMediaSource = null; // release app instance of AdaptiveMediaSource
47. 
48.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
49.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
50.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
51.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
52.             {
53.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
54.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
55. 
56.                 UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);
57. 
58.                 mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
59.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
60.             }
61.         }

63.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
64.         {
65.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
66.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
67.             if (mpItem != null)
68.             {
69.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
70.             }
71.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
72.             if (source != null)
73.             {
74.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
75.             }
76. 
77.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
78.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
79. 
80.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
81.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
82. 
83.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
84.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
85. 
86.             UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
90.         {
91.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
92. 
93.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
94.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, if the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box:
97.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
98.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
99.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
100. 
101.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
102.                 mediaPlayer,
103.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
104.                 null,
105.                 LoggerControl,
106.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
107. 
108.             // There is no InboundBitsPerSecondChanged event, so we start a polling thread to update UI.
109.             PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh);
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(CancellationTokenSource cts)
113.         {
114.             ulong InboundBitsPerSecondLast = 0;
115.             var refreshRate = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(2);
116.             while (!cts.IsCancellationRequested)
117.             {
118.                 if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
119.                 {
120.                     if (InboundBitsPerSecondLast != adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond)
121.                     {
122.                         InboundBitsPerSecondLast = adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond;
123.                         InboundBitsPerSecondText.Text = InboundBitsPerSecondLast.ToString();
124.                     }
125.                 }
126.                 await Task.Delay(refreshRate);
127.             }
128.         }

133.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
134.         {
135.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
136. 
137.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
138.             if (httpClient != null)
139.             {
140.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
141.             }
142.             else
143.             {
144.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
145.             }
146. 
147.             MediaSource source;
148.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
149.             {
150.                 adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
151. 
152.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
153.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
154. 
155.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
156.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
157. 
158.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
159.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
160.                 InitializeBitrateLists(adaptiveMediaSource);
161.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
162.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
163. 
164.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
165.             }
166.             else
167.             {
168.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
169.                 return null;
170.             }
171. 
172.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
173.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
174. 
175.             // Save the original Uri.
176.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
177. 
178.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
179.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
180. 
181.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
182. 
183.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
184.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
185. 
186.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
187. 
188.             return mpItem;
189.         }
190. 
191.         private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
192.         {
193.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
194.             // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
195.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
196.             {
197.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
198.             });
199.         }

205.         private void InitializeBitrateLists(AdaptiveMediaSource aMS)
206.         {
207.             var sortedBitrates = aMS.AvailableBitrates.OrderByDescending(br => br).Select(br => new BitrateItem(br)).ToArray();
208.             InitialBitrateList.ItemsSource = sortedBitrates;
209.             var selected = sortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
210.             InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = sortedBitrates.FirstOrDefault(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
211. 
212.             var nullableSortedBitrates = (new BitrateItem[] { new BitrateItem(null) }).Concat(sortedBitrates).ToArray();
213.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.ItemsSource = DesiredMinBitrateList.ItemsSource = nullableSortedBitrates;
214. 
215.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMaxBitrate);
216.             DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMinBitrate);
217.         }
218. 
219.         // An argument exception will be thrown if the following constraint is not met:
220.         //   DesiredMinBitrate <= InitialBitrate <= DesiredMaxBitrate
221.         private bool IsValidBitrateCombination(uint? desiredMinBitrate, uint? desiredMaxBitrate, uint initialBitrate)
222.         {
223.             // The ">" operator returns false if either operand is null. We take advantage of this
224.             // by testing in this manner. Do NOT "optimize" this by reversing the sense of the test
225.             // to "return desiredMinBitrate <= initialBitrate && initialBitrate <= desiredMaxBitrate;"
226.             if (desiredMinBitrate > initialBitrate || initialBitrate > desiredMaxBitrate)
227.             {
228.                 return false;
229.             }
230.             return true;
231.         }
232. 
233.         private void InitialBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
234.         {
235.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
236.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
237.             {
238.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, selection.Bitrate.Value))
239.                 {
240.                     adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate = selection.Bitrate.Value;
241.                 }
242.                 else
243.                 {
244.                     InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
245.                 }
246.             }
247.         }
248. 
249.         private void DesiredMinBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
250.         {
251.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
252.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
253.             {
254.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
255.                 {
256.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
257.                 }
258.                 else
259.                 {
260.                     DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
261.                 }
262.             }
263.         }
264. 
265.         private void DesiredMaxBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
266.         {
267.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
268.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
269.             {
270.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
271.                 {
272.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
273.                 }
274.                 else
275.                 {
276.                     DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
277.                 }
278.             }
279.         }

303.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
304.         {
305.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;

307.         }

309.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
310.         {
311.             if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
312.             {
313.                 adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
314.                 adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
315.             }
316.         }
317.         private async void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
318.         {
319.             uint downloadBitrate = args.NewValue;
320.             await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(downloadBitrate);
321.         }
322. 
323.         private async Task UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(uint downloadBitrate)
324.         {
325.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
326.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
327.             {
328.                 iconDownloadBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(downloadBitrate);
329.                 txtDownloadBitrate.Text = downloadBitrate.ToString();
330.             }));
331.         }
332.

334.         {
335.             uint playbackBitrate = args.NewValue;
336.             await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(playbackBitrate);
337.         }

364.     /// <summary>
365.     /// Item which provides a nicer display name for "null" bitrate.
366.     /// </summary>
367.     class BitrateItem
368.     {
369.         public uint? Bitrate { get; private set; }
370.         public BitrateItem(uint? bitrate)
371.         {
372.             Bitrate = bitrate;
373.         }
374. 
375.         public override string ToString()
376.         {
377.             return (Bitrate == null) ? "Not set" : Bitrate.ToString();
378.         }

39.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {

44.             if (mediaPlayer != null)

56.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
57.         {
58.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
59.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
60.             if (mpItem != null)
61.             {
62.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
63.                 UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents(mpItem.Source);
64.                 UnregisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(mpItem);
65.             }
66.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
67.             if (source != null)
68.             {
69.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
70.                 UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents(source);
71.             }
72. 
73.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
74.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
75. 
76.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
77.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
78. 
79.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
80.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
81. 
82.         }

84.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)

107.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
108.         {
109.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
110. 
111.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
112.             if (httpClient != null)
113.             {
114.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
115.             }
116.             else
117.             {
118.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
119.             }
120. 
121.             MediaSource source;
122.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
123.             {
124.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
125. 
126.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
127.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
128. 
129.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
130. 
131.             }
132.             else
133.             {
134.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
135.                 return null;
136.             }
137. 
138.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
139.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
140. 
141.             // Save the original Uri.
142.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
143. 
144.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
145.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "MainContent_ID";
146. 
147.             // In addition to logging, this scenario uses MediaSource events:
148.             RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(source);
149. 
150.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
151. 
152.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
153.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
154. 
155.             // In addition to logging, this scenario uses MediaPlaybackItem events:
156.             RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(mpItem);
157. 
158.             // Now that we should have a MediaPlaybackItem, we will insert pre- mid- and post-roll ads
159.             // with the MediaBreak API.
160.             CreateMediaBreaksForItem(mpItem);
161. 
162.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
163. 
164.             return mpItem;
165.         }

167.         private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
168.         {
169.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
